<a id='question-050'></a>

### Question 050: Integration: Resting Membrane Potential Calculation using Chord Conductance
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The resting membrane potential (RMP) of a cell is determined by the relative permeability (conductance) of the membrane to different ions and their respective equilibrium potentials, as described by the Goldman-Hodgkin-Katz (GHK) equation (or its simplified form, the chord conductance equation). (细胞静息电位由膜对不同离子的相对通透性（电导）及其各自的平衡电位决定，如Goldman-Hodgkin-Katz (GHK) 方程（或其简化形式，弦电导方程）所示。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
Dr. Anya Sharma, a neurophysiologist specializing in computational neuroscience, is developing a novel artificial neuron model for simulating complex neural circuits. She constructs an artificial excitable cell using lipid vesicles encapsulating ion channels and ionophores. To characterize the cell's electrical properties, she measures the membrane potential using microelectrodes. She determines the following parameters for the artificial cell at rest: the total conductance (g_total) is 100 pS. The individual conductances are: potassium conductance (g_K) = 80 pS, sodium conductance (g_Na) = 5 pS, and chloride conductance (g_Cl) = 15 pS. The equilibrium potentials (reversal potentials) for these ions are: potassium (E_K) = -90 mV, sodium (E_Na) = +60 mV, and chloride (E_Cl) = -70 mV. Using the chord conductance equation to calculate the resting membrane potential (E_m), what value should Dr. Sharma expect?

(A) -90 mV
(B) +60 mV
(C) -70 mV
(D) -79.5 mV
(E) -85 mV

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Physiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- The vignette describes an artificial cell with known ion conductances (g_K, g_Na, g_Cl) and equilibrium potentials (E_K, E_Na, E_Cl).
- The total conductance (g_total) is the sum of individual conductances: g_total = g_K + g_Na + g_Cl = 80 pS + 5 pS + 15 pS = 100 pS.
- The question asks for the calculation of the resting membrane potential (E_m) using the chord conductance equation.
- The chord conductance equation is a simplified form of the GHK equation, representing the membrane potential as a weighted average of the equilibrium potentials, where the weights are the relative conductances: E_m = (g_K * E_K + g_Na * E_Na + g_Cl * E_Cl) / g_total.
- The key is to correctly substitute the given values into the equation and perform the calculation.

##### 2. Physiological & Molecular Deep-Dive (生理机制、微循环动力学与神经体液调节深度剖析)
- The resting membrane potential (RMP) is the electrical potential difference across the plasma membrane of a cell when it is not stimulated or excited.
- It is primarily determined by the differential distribution of ions across the membrane and the selective permeability of the membrane to these ions.
- The Goldman-Hodgkin-Katz (GHK) equation provides a quantitative description of the RMP, considering the contributions of multiple ions:
  E_m = (RT/F) * ln[(P_K[K+]_out + P_Na[Na+]_out + P_Cl[Cl-]_in) / (P_K[K+]_in + P_Na[Na+]_in + P_Cl[Cl-]_out)]
  Where:
    - E_m is the membrane potential.
    - R is the ideal gas constant.
    - T is the absolute temperature.
    - F is the Faraday constant.
    - P represents the permeability of the membrane to the ion (proportional to conductance, g).
    - [ ]_out and [ ]_in denote extracellular and intracellular concentrations, respectively.
- The chord conductance equation is a simplified version often used when the concentrations are assumed to be constant or when dealing with relative conductances:
  E_m = (g_K * E_K + g_Na * E_Na + g_Cl * E_Cl) / (g_K + g_Na + g_Cl) = (g_K * E_K + g_Na * E_Na + g_Cl * E_Cl) / g_total
- In this specific case:
  - g_K = 80 pS, E_K = -90 mV
  - g_Na = 5 pS, E_Na = +60 mV
  - g_Cl = 15 pS, E_Cl = -70 mV
  - g_total = 100 pS
- Substituting these values into the chord conductance equation:
  E_m = (80 * -90 + 5 * +60 + 15 * -70) / 100
  E_m = (-7200 + 300 - 1050) / 100
  E_m = (-7950) / 100
  E_m = -79.5 mV
- This calculation shows that the resting membrane potential is heavily influenced by the high potassium conductance and its equilibrium potential, but also receives contributions (albeit smaller) from sodium and chloride conductances. The negative value indicates that the inside of the cell is negative relative to the outside at rest.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) -90 mV: This is the equilibrium potential for potassium (E_K). While potassium conductance is dominant (80%), it's not the *only* conductance. The presence of Na+ and Cl- conductances pulls the membrane potential slightly positive compared to E_K. This option ignores the contributions of Na+ and Cl-. Students might choose this if they incorrectly assume the RMP is solely determined by the ion with the highest conductance.
- (B) +60 mV: This is the equilibrium potential for sodium (E_Na). The resting membrane has very low sodium conductance (5%). Therefore, the RMP cannot be positive and equal to E_Na. This option reflects a misunderstanding of the relative conductances at rest. Students might choose this if they confuse E_Na with E_m or incorrectly assume Na+ dominates.
- (C) -70 mV: This is the equilibrium potential for chloride (E_Cl). Chloride conductance is 15%, which is non-negligible but less than potassium. The RMP is more negative than E_Cl because of the dominant influence of potassium. This option might be chosen if students focus only on the chloride contribution or miscalculate the weighted average.
- (E) -85 mV: This value is closer to the correct answer but is incorrect. It might result from a calculation error, perhaps misinterpreting the relative weights or equilibrium potentials. For example, if g_Na was 10% instead of 5%, the calculation would be ((80*-90)+(10*+60)+(15*-70))/100 = (-7200+600-1050)/100 = -7650/100 = -76.5 mV. Or if g_Cl was 5% instead of 15%, the calculation would be ((80*-90)+(5*+60)+(5*-70))/100 = (-7200+300-350)/100 = -7250/100 = -72.5 mV. This option tests careful calculation.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- The resting membrane potential is a weighted average of the equilibrium potentials of permeable ions, with the weights being the relative conductances. (静息电位是可渗透离子的平衡电位的加权平均值，权重是相对电导。)
- The GHK equation (or chord conductance equation) accurately predicts the RMP given the conductances and equilibrium potentials. (GHK 方程（或弦电导方程）可以根据电导和平衡电位准确预测静息电位。)
- In most neurons, the high resting permeability to K+ makes the RMP close to E_K, but other ions contribute. (在大多数神经元中，高静息状态下对 K+ 的通透性使静息电位接近 E_K，但其他离子也有贡献。)
- Differential: Action potentials involve rapid changes in Na+ and K+ conductances, causing large deviations from the RMP. (差异：动作电位涉及 Na+ 和 K+ 电导的快速变化，导致静息电位产生较大偏差。)

---
